MCP Time Tracker @theluckystrike/mcp-time-tracker

Track billable time without leaving the chat.

What it does. Starts and stops timers, records manual entries, sets hourly rates per project, and turns the log into reports grouped by project, day, task or tag, CSV exports and invoice-ready line items. MCP Deposits @theluckystrike/mcp-deposits

Security and retainer deposits, held per client, on the same engine as your invoices.

What it does. Records a security or retainer deposit when the money arrives, in minor units and in the currency it arrived in, and then answers for it: what is held per client and per currency, since when, and what has already gone. Applying a deposit to an invoice writes the payment onto that invoice under both locks, adding to what was already paid rather than replacing it, so a bank transfer that landed first is not erased; it can never pay out more than the deposit still holds or more than the invoice still owes, and a deposit is never converted between currencies. Refunds hand money back without touching the invoice, the stored status is derived from the movements every time one is written, and the statement is one client in one currency, as text or as an A4 PDF that matches the invoice and credit note next to it.

Who it is for. Anyone who takes money before the work: landlords and letting agents on security deposits, agencies and consultants on retainers, trades on up-front payments.

Example. "Record a 500 euro security deposit from Nordic Print, received today." ... "Apply 300 of that deposit to INV-2026-0001." ... "How much are we holding for Nordic Print?" ... "Refund the rest, paid back by bank transfer."

MCP Per Diem @theluckystrike/mcp-per-diem

Statutory travel allowances on the rate tables the tax authorities publish, and the trips you priced with them.

What it does. Prices one business trip against a bundled rate table and shows the arithmetic: the allowance per day, the partial-day fraction the scheme's own ladder produced, every deduction for a meal somebody else paid for, and the total in the scheme's own currency. Three schemes ship with it: the Polish delegation regulation at home and per country, the HMRC benchmark scale rates for travel inside the UK, and the US GSA CONUS standard M&IE with the lodging cap. Start and end are instants rather than wall clocks, so a trip across a clock change is 23 or 25 hours and the Polish 24-hour period and the US calendar day give different, correct answers to the same trip. Trips can be saved under a name with a TRIP-YYYY-NNNN id, listed with a total per currency, reported per scheme and per month, and handed to the expense tracker as ready-made expense_add arguments. The tables are files inside the package, not a feed, so the same trip priced twice gives the same answer, and every rate carries its authority, instrument, source URL and effective date.

Who it is for. Anyone who claims or pays a statutory travel allowance: Polish contractors and employers on delegacja and diety, UK sole traders and companies on the HMRC benchmark rates, and US federal or corporate travellers on the GSA standard.

Example. "What is the per diem for 58 hours in Krakow, leaving Tuesday 8am, with breakfast on the first day and two nights in a hotel?" ... "Same trip under the HMRC rates." ... "Save it as the Krakow audit trip." ... "Total my per diems this quarter per scheme."

FreeRate lookups and calculations are unlimited on every tier: the tables are public regulation, and metering the reading of a regulation would be charging for the tax authority's work. 5 trips saved per calendar month, counted by start date, and unlimited trip lists.Pro $19Unlimited trips saved, the expense-tracker export payloads (one per currency, ready for expense_add), and the report of totals per scheme and per calendar month. BuyData stays~/.local/share/mcp-servers/per-diem/ Installclaude mcp add per-diem -- npx -y @theluckystrike/mcp-per-diem Statusnpm missing registry pending github published validation 38/38
ToolWhat it does
perdiem_ratesList a scheme's bundled daily allowance rates with the authority, instrument, source URL and effective date they came from. Filter by country or city. Free and unlimited.
perdiem_calcCalculate the daily travel allowance for one trip: the amount per day and the total in the scheme's currency, with the partial-day fraction and the meal deductions the scheme's own rule applies. Free and unlimited.
trip_recordCalculate a trip and save it under a name, with the traveller taken from the shared business profile. Returns the TRIP-YYYY-NNNN id. Free tier: 5 trips a calendar month.

perdiem_reportTotal the saved trips per scheme and per calendar month, in each scheme's own currency, with the day count and the trips behind every figure. Pro.

README · build sprint · source

MCP Asset Register @theluckystrike/mcp-asset-register

A fixed asset register that depreciates on the rates the tax authorities actually publish, with the schedule, the journal and the gain on sale.

What it does. Keeps a register of what you bought, what it cost, when it went into use and which category it falls in, and returns the rate, the useful life, the convention, the full schedule year by year or month by month, one month's journal entry, and the gain or loss when you sell. Three tables ship with it: the Polish annual rates from the annex to the CIT and PIT acts keyed to the KST classification (33 rows, with declining-balance eligibility and the art. 16k switch to straight line), the UK capital allowance pools at 18 and 6 percent with the annual investment allowance, and the US MACRS GDS half-year percentages for 3, 5 and 7 year property exactly as IRS Pub 946 Table A-1 publishes them. The convention is the table's, not the caller's: Poland charges from the month AFTER the asset enters the register, so an asset in service on 15 March starts on 1 April and year one is nine twelfths, while the MACRS percentages already carry the half-year convention and a UK writing down allowance is not prorated by month at all. The same asset gives three different year-one figures under the three schemes and all three are right. Money is integer minor units end to end and the schedule is allocated by cumulative rounding, so the periods sum to cost less residual to the minor unit and the months sum to their year. The tables are files inside the package, not a feed, and every rate carries its authority, instrument, source URL and effective date.

Who it is for. Anyone who has to put a depreciation figure on a return or in a ledger: Polish companies and sole traders on the KST annex rates, UK businesses claiming capital allowances on the main and special rate pools, and US filers on MACRS GDS.

Example. "Add the Dell workstation, 8,499 zloty, in service 15 March 2026, computers." ... "Show me the schedule month by month." ... "Depreciate the same truck under declining balance." ... "What is April's journal entry?" ... "We sold the van for 12,000 in September, what is the gain?"

FreeDepreciation schedules are unlimited on every tier: the rates are public information a tax authority published, and metering the reading of a regulation would be charging for the government's work. The cap is on the SIZE of the register, 10 assets, plus unlimited asset_list and asset_dispose.Pro $19Unlimited assets in the register, the monthly journal entry with its ready-made expense_add payload (one per currency, no vat_rate because depreciation is a book charge), and the report of net book value by category and currency with the year's charge and every disposal in it. BuyData stays~/.local/share/mcp-servers/asset-register/ Installclaude mcp add asset-register -- npx -y @theluckystrike/mcp-asset-register Statusnpm missing registry pending github published validation 38/38
ToolWhat it does
asset_addAdd one fixed asset to the register and return its id with the rate, useful life and convention taken from the bundled tax table. Cost and residual are whole minor units. Free tier holds ten assets.
asset_listList the assets in the register with cost, method, rate and net book value at a date, filtered by scheme, category, project, currency or disposal state. Free and unlimited.
asset_scheduleBuild the depreciation schedule for a stored asset, or price one not yet in the register, per year or per month, to zero or residual, with the table convention applied and the periods summing exactly to the base. Free.
asset_journalReturn the depreciation journal for one month: debit depreciation expense and credit accumulated depreciation, per asset and in total, plus an expense_add-ready payload per currency. It writes nothing itself. Pro.
asset_disposeRecord the sale, scrapping or write-off of an asset on a date for a proceeds amount, and return the gain or loss against net book value at that date. Free.
asset_reportReport net book value by category and currency at a date, the year's depreciation charge, and every disposal in the year with its gain or loss. Pro.

README · build sprint · source

MCP Statement of Account @theluckystrike/mcp-statement-of-account

The one document that answers what a client actually owes you, aged as at any date, with the chaser drafted.

What it does. Reads the three books this suite already keeps -- the invoice ledger, the credit notes and the deposits -- and turns them into a statement of account for a period: the balance carried at the start, every invoice issued, every payment received, every credit note given, the deposits applied broken out of the receipts rather than added again, and the closing balance. The movements are in date order and the opening balance plus their sum equals the closing balance, so the document is auditable line by line. statement_aging splits what is still open into 0-30, 31-60, 61-90 and over 90 days past the due date AS AT the date asked for, which means an invoice issued after that date is not on the books, a payment made after it has not happened and a credit note issued after it has not been given. Due today is not overdue: day zero sits in a not-yet-due line reported beside the four buckets. paid_minor on the invoice is the authority and the payment rows are only the attribution, so a deposit application that raised paid_minor without writing a payment row still appears. It writes into no book it reports on: bytes and mtimes of the sibling stores are unchanged across all six tools, and the only file it owns is a register of the statements it built. Nothing here invents a late fee, an interest rate or a legal cost, and a dunning letter escalates in tone and never in figures.

Who it is for. Anyone who invoices and then has to chase: freelancers and small firms who need to send a client a statement rather than forwarding five invoices, and anyone who has to answer what was owed on a given date rather than what is owed now.

Example. "Send Acme a statement for June." ... "Who owes me money, aged." ... "What was outstanding on 10 June?" ... "Draft a firm chaser for Acme." ... "Show me every client, per currency, with the oldest overdue invoice."

Freestatement_aging is free and unlimited on every tier, for one client or for everyone: who owes me money is the question this server exists for, and a free tier that hides it is a demo. The meter is on the document that goes to a client, five distinct statements a calendar month, counted by client, period and currency, so rebuilding one already in the register is free forever. Plain-text statements and dunning at the friendly and firm levels are free.Pro $19Unlimited statements, the A4 PDF on the same page layout as your invoices with your logo and no footer credit, the level 3 final demand, and statements_report: every client at once, totalled per currency, ranked by what is overdue rather than by what is large, with the oldest overdue invoice in the whole book named. ToolWhat it does
statement_buildBuild one client's statement of account for a period: opening balance, invoices issued, payments received, credit notes, deposits applied and closing balance, in minor units and formatted.
statement_agingBreak what is owed into 0-30, 31-60, 61-90 and over 90 days past the invoice due date as at a chosen date, for one client or for every client, per currency. Free and unlimited.
statement_textTurn one client's statement into a plain-text ledger with every movement in date order, the opening and closing balances and a sign-off, ready to paste into an email.

dunning_textWrite a payment chaser for one client at level 1 friendly, 2 firm or 3 final demand, listing every overdue invoice with its age and total, plus your bank details when the profile has them. Level 3 is Pro.

Reading the table

  • Measured beats estimated. Rows whose reach cell says MEASURED come from this estate's own numbers (X replies earned zero in 86% of 397 cases; LinkedIn buyer share 29-47%; estate clicks ~1,632/28d). Rows marked estimate are ranked below any measured row of similar size.
  • Registries compound, posts decay. A registry or marketplace entry keeps producing installs for months; a social post is a one-day spike. Every row above impact 60 is a registry, a marketplace, or a search surface.
  • The pipeline stops at the human steps. Four rows need one click each (npm login, Smithery, Glama, extensions directory). Each is under a minute and unlocks a terminal-only surface behind it.
  • Budget rule. After two weeks, keep only channels with at least one measured click to mcp.zovo.one or one install; drop the rest.

Invoice PDF

A4 single page, clean grid. Header (business name left, INVOICE + number right), issue/due/status block, BILL TO, 4-column line table with per-line tax, right-aligned Subtotal/Tax/Total with Total in EUR, payment details with IBAN and reference, free-tier footer. Nothing cut off, nothing overlapping, totals readable at 80 dpi. Two cosmetic gaps: line amounts carry no currency symbol (only the Total does), and BILL TO shows only the client name because invoice_create auto-created Acme with no address.

Free tier in a first session

  • spreadsheet: 200-row write cap at sheet_add_column on a 400-row sheet (high). Message is clear but the tool still reports success and writes a file that looks complete. Data loss risk.
  • price-tracker: alerts_pending is Pro-only at "whether anything dropped" (high). Blocks the core promise of a price tracker on free.
  • invoice: overdue_report is Pro-only at "which invoices are overdue" (medium). invoice_list covers it, so the free user still gets an answer.

Agent units (RESULT.md files)

The per-unit table with cost, failures and insights is on the Overview tab under Sprint results.

What an MCP product owner should measure, in funnel order: discovered, installed, first call, value delivered, reliable, paid. Every indicator names its instrument so it can be re-collected with node scripts/kpi.mjs. Collected 2026-09-05T17:11:18.719Z. Status: met = at or past target; progress = non-zero and below target; zero = instrument works and reads zero; unmeasured = instrument not yet wired.

Click to checkout-session ratio26.8 % (sessions per 100 clicks)40progress100 * checkout sessions from humans (last 100, Stripe) / upgrade link clicks (7d, /stats/clicks) - the two windows differ (last 100 ever vs last 7 days), so this is an approximation, not the true window-matched ratioSeparates whether people are seeing the link (clicks) from whether the checkout page itself converts (ratio); a link with clicks and no sessions points at the redirect or the Stripe page, not the message.
Pro tenants on hosted endpoints0 tenants20zeroStripe-bound tenants (REMOTE_DATA bind: prefix, 0) plus distinct lic: tenant ids whose key the billing worker minted (LICENSES session:*, 0 keys). EXCLUDED: the 1590 raw lic: documents are per (key, server) pairs, not tenants (193 distinct ids), and 193 of those ids are validation probes signed locally by scripts/sign-license.mjs, never bought.Real Pro usage on hosted. The old count read every lic: document, so one validation run over 16 servers looked like 16 paying tenants.

Reading the KPIs as a specialist

  • Hosted activation is the leading indicator. Registries and directories only count hosted use, and an anonymous token is the cheapest possible first call. Watch anon tokens and tenant documents before downloads.
  • Tool reach beats tool count. A server that is correct on direct probes but loses to a built-in reader delivers nothing; the first-prompt reach rate is the number to protect, and descriptions are its lever.
  • Probes contaminate funnel metrics. Validation runs create checkout sessions and Pro tenants; since 2026-09-03 they are tagged (metadata.probe, signed keys) and excluded. Pre-tag sessions are excluded by timestamp.
  • Latency has a cliff, not a curve. Round 4 measured first-prompt misses when connect plus tools/list ran past about a second; p50 is tracked against 800 ms.
  • Until paid sessions are non-zero, every other number is a proxy. The KPI that closes the loop is paid sessions and minted keys; the rest exist to explain why it is still zero.

Organic score = 100 x listed x findable x reach. listed: 1 live, 0.5 PR/submission pending, 0.3-0.2 blocked on a human/CI step, 0 skipped/not published. findable for registry-backed surfaces is MEASURED via fully-paginated /v0/servers?search=<token> calls (limit=100, cursor loop -- fixes a prior single-page undercount risk): p(seen|query) = 0 if our current-version row for THAT server does not appear, else min(1, 10/rank), using the row's own best rank restricted to that server's registry name(s) (a token like 'price' that also substring-matches a different server's long slug, e.g. office-suite, is NOT credited to the wrong server). findable = mean of p over 56 natural query slots across all nine servers' query_set (was 41 across six servers before currency/docx/timezone existed). Per-server organic = 100 x mean(p) over that server's own query set. 'Hosted endpoints' findable = share of the 56 query slots whose matched row belongs to a server that now carries a non-empty remotes[] (8 of 9 servers: everything except office-suite). fleet.fleet_score is a noisy-OR over the nine per-server organics (100 x (1 - product(1 - p_i/100))), applied identically before and after for comparability.

Regenerate: node scripts/validate.mjs && node scripts/update-dashboard.mjs --note "..." && node scripts/render-main.mjs. Data: data/ledger.json, data/facts.json, data/distribution.json. Built by theluckystrike. https://github.com/theluckystrike
